1-10 月进出口数据点评

高技术产业支撑海内外需求   
10 月高技术产业继续支撑海外需求。10 月主要贸易方对我国当月出口同比增
速的正贡献均有收窄。运价等成本升高或继续影响我国劳动密集型产品出口
竞争力。 
 11 月 10 日，海关总署公布数据显示，以美元计价，2026 年 1-10 月，我
国货物贸易出口金额（以下简称： “出口”）同比增长22.7%，增幅（较
1-9 月，下同）走扩 1.4 个百分点；货物贸易进口金额（以下简称：“进
口”）同比增长 29.0%，增幅走扩 1.5 个百分点；当期贸易顺差 10425.9
亿美元。 以人民币计价，1-10 月我国出口同比增长 17.6%， 进口同比增长
23.6%；贸易顺差为 71632.6 亿元。 
单月数据方面，以美元计价，10 月我国出口同比增长 35.2%，增速较 9
月小幅放缓 1.1 个百分点；10 月进口同比增长 41.6%， 增幅较9 月明显走
扩 10.6 个百分点；贸易顺差 1052.8 亿美元。以人民币计价，10 月我国出
口同比增长 28.7%， 增速较9 月收窄 0.9 个百分点； 进口同比增长34.7%，
增速明显加快 10.1 个百分点。从单月环比数据看，10 月我国出口当月环
比下降 7.8%； 进口当月环比下降2.2%。 受到国庆假期的影响，10 月出口、
进口环比有所下滑，但同比增速仍维持较高水平，我国外贸景气度维持
扩张。 
 高技术产业继续支撑海外需求。首先，10 月欧元区、美国 PMI 数据分别
为 52.7%和 55.5%， 均仍处于较高景气度区间；10 月韩国、 越南当月出口
同比增速分别为 93.7%和 53.8%， 反映出当前海外需求的较强韧性， 带动
我国出口表现。其次，高技术制造业需求维持强韧，支撑我国出口同比
增速表现。得益于全球“AI”等高技术制造业投资旺盛，10 月当月我国
部分机电产品出口同比继续较快增长，集成电路出口当月同比增速为
137.1%， 增速较9 月加快 1.1 百分点， 仍维持较明显的正增长， 全球高技
术产业加速发展有望对外需持续形成支撑。 
 此外， 高技术制造业或也对我国进口表现有所支撑；9 月高技术产业工业
增加值同比增长 15.3%，维持较高水平增速；10 月高技术制造业 PMI 指
数 51.3%， 仍维持在扩张区间； 国内高技术产业增长带动相关产品进口增
速表现，10 月我国集成电路进口当月同比增速为 78.3%，增速较 9 月加
快 3.6 个百分点。 
 运价等成本升高或继续影响我国劳动密集型产品出口竞争力。 出口方
面，首先，1-10 月集成电路及自动数据处理设备出口金额同比实现较快
增速 ， 同比增速分别为111.1%和 60.6%， 海外高技术产品需求仍较旺盛。 
其次，当期机械设备出口表现较好，通用设备、船舶及汽车（和底盘）
出口同比增速分别为 99.5%、27.7%和 52.2 %；值得注意的是，汽车相关
产品出口额在近年出口较高基数的基础上，维持较快增速，我国汽车行
业出口优势仍较明显。 
此外，劳动密集型产品出口仍相对弱势， 1-10 月多数轻工业产品出口金
额同比增速低于当期出口同比增速整体水平，鞋靴、灯具及家用陶瓷器
皿出口同比负增长；我们认为，运价等成本升高或继续影响我国劳动密
集型产品出口竞争力。 
 进口方面，1-10 月，我国集成电路和自动数据处理设备及其零部件同比
分别增长 65.1%和 136.7%。 
 风险提示： 海外大宗商品价格震荡；地缘关系的不确定性； 政策效果不
及预期。

### Page 2

2026 年 11 月 10 日 1-10 月进出口数据点评 2

图表 1.进出口数据表现

资料来源：Wind，中银证券

11 月 10 日，海关总署公布数据显示，以美元计价，2026 年 1-10 月，我国货物贸易出口金额（以下
简称：“出口”）同比增长 22.7%，增幅（较 1-9 月，下同）走扩 1.4 个百分点；货物贸易进口金额
（以下简称：“进口”）同比增长 29.0%，增幅走扩 1.5 个百分点；当期贸易顺差 10425.9 亿美元。
以人民币计价，1-10 月我国出口同比增长 17.6%，进口同比增长 23.6%；贸易顺差为 71632.6 亿元。 
单月数据方面，以美元计价，10 月我国出口同比增长 35.2%，增速较 9 月小幅放缓 1.1 个百分点；
10 月进口同比增长 41.6%，增幅较 9 月明显走扩 10.6 个百分点；贸易顺差 1052.8 亿美元。以人民币
计价，10 月我国出口同比增长 28.7%，增速较 9 月收窄 0.9 个百分点；进口同比增长 34.7%，增速明
显加快 10.1 个百分点。从单月环比数据看，10 月我国出口当月环比下降 7.8%；进口当月环比下降
2.2%。受到国庆假期的影响，10 月出口、进口环比有所下滑，但同比增速仍维持较高水平，我国外
贸景气度维持扩张。 
高技术产业继续支撑海外需求。首先，10 月欧元区、美国 PMI 数据分别为 52.7%和 55.5%，均仍处
于较高景气度区间；10 月韩国、越南当月出口同比增速分别为 93.7%和 53.8%，反映出当前海外需
求的较强韧性，带动我国出口表现。其次，高技术制造业需求维持强韧， 支撑我国出口同比增速表
现。得益于全球“AI”等高技术制造业投资旺盛，10 月当月我国部分机电产品出口同比继续较快增
长，集成电路出口当月同比增速为 137.1%，增速较 9 月加快 1.1 百分点，仍维持较明显的正增长，
全球高技术产业加速发展有望对外需持续形成支撑。 
此外，高技术制造业或也对我国进口表现有所支撑；9 月高技术产业工业增加值同比增长 15.3%，维
持较高水平增速；10 月高技术制造业 PMI 指数 51.3%，仍维持在扩张区间；国内高技术产业增长带
动相关产品进口增速表现，10 月我国集成电路进口当月同比增速为 78.3%，增速较 9 月加快 3.6 个
百分点。 
主要贸易方对我国当月出口同比增速的正贡献均有收窄。分国别看，1-10 月我国对美国、欧盟、东
盟 、 日本及非洲等贸易伙伴的出口同比增速均为正 ， 其中对东盟和非洲的出口同比增速分别为 30.5%
和 24.0%，“一带一路”国家对我国外需的支撑仍较明显；此外，同期对美出口同比增速为12.1%，
增幅较 1-9 月走扩 2.3 个百分点。 
从单月数据表现看 ，10 月东盟进出口总额实现 1240.8 亿美元 ， 占比17.2%； 其中 ， 出口同比增长 46.3%，
进口同比增长 42.0%；10 月我国对欧盟进出口总额 758.0 亿美元，占比 10.5%；其中，出口同比增长
24.3%， 进口同比下滑 4.2%；对美国进出口总额 601.2 亿美元 ， 占比8.4%， 其中 ， 出口同比增长 32.9%，
进口同比增长 35.0%。从贡献角度看，10 月东盟、欧盟及美国对当期出口同比增速的正贡献分别为
8.1、3.5 和 3.8 个百分点；10 月以上主要贸易方对我国出口同比增速的正贡献均有缩窄，东盟、欧
盟及美国正贡献分别收窄 0.4、0.2 和 0.4 个百分点。

### Page 3

2026 年 11 月 10 日 1-10 月进出口数据点评 3

图表 2. 1-10 月主要产品的出口同比增速  图表 3. 1-10 月主要产品的进口同比增速

资料来源：Wind，中银证券  资料来源：Wind，中银证券

运价等成本升高或继续影响我国劳动密集型产品出口竞争力 。出口方面，首先，1-10 月集成电路及
自动数据处理设备出口金额同比实现较快增速，同比增速分别为 111.1%和 60.6%，海外高技术产品
需求仍较旺盛。 
其次，当期机械设备出口表现较好，通用设备、船舶及汽车（和底盘） 出口同比增速分别为99.5%、
27.7%和 52.2 %； 值得注意的是 ，汽车相关产品出口额在近年出口较高基数的基础上 ，维持较快增速，
我国汽车行业出口优势仍较明显。 
此外，劳动密集型产品出口仍相对弱势，1-10 月多数轻工业产品出口金额同比增速低于当期出口同
比增速整体水平，鞋靴、灯具及家用陶瓷器皿出口同比负增长；我们认为，运价等成本升高或继续
影响我国劳动密集型产品出口竞争力。 
进口方面 ， 1-10 月 ， 我国集成电路和自动数据处理设备及其零部件同比分别增长65.1%和 136.7%。

图表 4. 1-10 月主要出口产品价格同比增速表现   图表 5. 1-10 月主要进口产品价格同比增速表现

资料来源：Wind，中银证券  资料来源：Wind，中银证券

价格因素对 10 月出口同比增速的影响仍较明显。1-10 月我国高技术产品进、出口相对活跃，集成电
路进出口均维持较高的累计同比增速；而从价格表现来看，1-10 月集成电路进口、出口价格同比增
速分别为 58.6%和 107.9%，得益于海外、国内高技术产业投资持续发力，我国部分高技术产品进、
出口量价齐升。 
原材料方面，当前中东地区地缘政治冲突仍有反复，对国内上游产品进口需求有所影响。 据我们测
算，1-10 月原油 、成品油进口单价同比增速均高于 15.0%， 但两项产品进口数量同比降幅均高于 15.0%；
此外，同期铜矿砂（及其精矿）和未锻造的铜（及铜材） 的进口单价同比分别增长32.2%和 36.6%，
但以上两种产品同期进口数量也同比下滑，国内工业特别是制造业上游采购需求或仍受到影响。 
风险提示：海外大宗商品价格震荡；地缘关系的不确定性；政策效果不及预期。

披露声明 
本报告准确表述了证券分析师的个人观点。该证券分析师声明，本人未在公司内、外部机构兼任有损本人独立性与客
观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。  
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报
告有关情况。 如有投资者于未经授权的公众媒体看到或从其他机构获得本研究报告的， 请慎重使用所获得的研究报告，
以防止被误导，中银国际证券股份有限公司不对其报告理解和使用承担任何责任。  
评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

### Page 1

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9番 1号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー

2026年 10月 30日 全7頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月鉱工業生産 
自動車工業が挽回生産で持ち直し、生産全体は3カ月ぶりに上昇

経済調査部 エコノミスト ビリング 安奈

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 9月の生産指数は前月比＋2.2％と、コンセンサス（同＋1.2％、Bloomberg調
査）を上回り、3 カ月ぶりに上昇した。自動車工業は挽回生産で持ち直したほか、AI
関連需要で生産用機械工業なども増産し、生産全体を押し上げた。経済産業省は基調
判断を「一進一退」に据え置いた。 
◼ 先行きの生産指数は緩やかに上昇する見込み。AI関連需要などが引き続き国内生産を
押し上げよう。ただし、金利高による海外需要の下振れなどが生産を下押しするリス
クには注意が必要だ。 
◼ 2026年 11月 9日に公表予定の 9月分の景気動向指数は、先行 CIが前月差▲0.9ptの
117.1、一致CIが同＋0.7ptの 119.5と予想する。この予測値に基づくと、9月の基調
判断は機械的に「改善」に据え置かれる。

【生産】自動車工業は挽回生産で持ち直し、AI関連需要で生産用機械工業も増産 
2026年 9月の生産指数は前月比＋2.2％と、コンセンサス（同＋1.2％、Bloomberg調査）を
上回り、3カ月ぶりに上昇した。経済産業省は基調判断を「一進一退」に据え置いた。 
生産指数を業種別に見ると、15業種中 8業種が前月から上昇した。自動車工業（前月比＋
5.5％）は 2カ月ぶりに上昇した。品目別では、普通乗用車（同＋3.3％）や駆動伝導・操縦装
置部品（同＋7.0％）などが増産となった。前月は熊本地震や台風による工場稼働停止や部品
供給の停滞を受けて生産が落ち込んだが、9 月は生産体制がおおむね正常化したことで挽回生
産が進み、自動車工業の生産は持ち直した。また、生産用機械工業（同＋3.4％）は4カ月連続
で上昇した。品目別では、AI関連製品である半導体製造装置（同＋13.8％）のほか、化学機械
（同＋34.9％）などが増産となった。経済産業省「鉱工業出荷内訳表」によると、生産用機械
工業は国内向け（同＋7.8％）が好調だった。汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋4.8％）では、コン
ベヤ（同＋142.0％）や運搬用クレーン（同＋35.5％）が増産となり、2カ月ぶりの上昇となっ
た。一方、鉄鋼・非鉄金属工業（前月比▲1.6％）や窯業・土石製品工業（同▲1.9％）など 7
業種は前月から低下した。鉄鋼・非鉄金属工業では亜鉛めっき鋼板（同▲10.0％）などが、窯
業・土石製品工業ではガラス繊維（短繊維）（同▲6.3％）などが減産となった。 
財別では、生産財（前月比＋1.0％）、耐久消費財（同＋5.3％）、非耐久消費財（同＋0.9％）、
建設財（同＋2.5％）、資本財（除．輸送機械） （同＋1.0％）のいずれも増産となった。設備投
資の関連指標である資本財（除．輸送機械）はAI需要を背景に大幅な増産が見込まれる。

【出荷・在庫】出荷指数は自動車工業などを中心に2カ月ぶりに上昇 
2026年 9月の出荷指数は前月比＋0.6％と 2カ月ぶりに上昇した。業種別では、自動車工業
（同＋2.3％）や汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋5.5％）など 15業種中 8業種が上昇した。財別に
見ると、非耐久消費財と資本財（除．輸送機械）、建設財は低下した一方、耐久消費財と生産
財は上昇した。在庫指数は同＋2.5％、在庫率指数は同＋2.0％だった。

【先行き】生産指数は緩やかに上昇する見込み 
先行きの生産指数は、緩やかな上昇基調を辿るとみている。AI関連需要などが引き続き国内
生産の押し上げ要因となる。ただし、世界的な金利高、特に米国の金利高による海外需要の下
振れなどが生産を下押しするリスクには注意が必要だ。 
AI関連財の拡大は 2024年から日本国内の生産を押し上げてきており、今後も生産の押し上
げ要因になると見込まれる1。26年 8月の世界半導体販売額（3カ月平均）を見ると、前年比＋
144.3％と強い伸びが続いている。米国の販売額（同＋180.9％）がけん引する中、日本（同＋
62.3％）2も力強さを示している。また、日本半導体製造装置協会によると、26年9月の半導体
製造装置販売高（3カ月平均）は同＋48.2％と高い伸びとなった3。AI関連を中心とする旺盛な
半導体需要が反映されているとみられる。ただし、米国の金利高でAI関連需要が下振れする可
能性には注意が必要だ。高金利下で米企業の資金調達負担が増え、多額の資金を必要とする AI
関連投資が鈍化すれば4、日本の生産も輸出減を通して下押しされるリスクがある。 
なお、中東情勢による悪影響は引き続き注視する必要がある。米国の中間選挙後に対イラン
攻撃が再開される可能性も報じられており5、原油供給をめぐる不安は引き続き残る。一方、今
年 2月末に始まった中東危機を受け、ホルムズ海峡の輸送停滞への対応として政府が国家備蓄
原油を放出して以降、初めて買い戻しが実施された6。代替調達が進み、国家備蓄原油の取り崩
しが一服し、備蓄の補充が始まったことは好材料だ。ただし、情勢が再び悪化すれば、原材料
不足を通じて生産が下押しされる可能性がある。 
製造工業生産予測調査では、10月の生産指数は前月比＋5.1％と見込まれる。業種別では 11
業種中 9業種が上昇する見通しだ。電子部品・デバイス工業（同＋16.6％）で集積回路などの
増産が計画されているほか、生産用機械工業（同＋15.5％）では半導体・フラットパネルディ
スプレイ製造装置など、AI関連製品の増産が全体を押し上げる見通しだ。生産指数全体の計画
のバイアスを補正した試算値（最頻値）7で見ると、10月は同＋3.9％と上昇が見込まれる。 
11月の生産指数は前月比＋0.8％と見込まれている。業種別では 11業種中 7業種が上昇する
見通しだ。輸送機械工業（同＋4.3％）では乗用車など、汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋4.8％）
では計測・分析機器などの増産が見込まれている。

1 詳細は、神田慶司・秋元虹輝・菊池慈陽・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年6月」（大和総研レポート、
2026年6月23日）を参照。 
2 World Semiconductor Trade Statistics “Historical Billings Report”（最終閲覧日：2026年10月 30
日） 
3 日本半導体製造装置協会「2026年9月度販売高（SEAJ速報値）日本製半導体製造装置（3ヶ月平均）」
（2026年10月 23日） 
4 詳細は、矢作大祐・藤原翼「米国経済見通し CoreWeave問題をどうみるか」（大和総研レポート、2026年
10月22日）を参照。 
5 「トランプ政権が湾岸諸国に対イラン攻撃への準備を要請 米中間選挙後に最終判断か」（産経新聞、2026
年 10月 29日） 
6 「国家備蓄原油、中東危機後初の買い戻し UAE産を 200万バレル」（日本経済新聞 電子版、2026年10月
28日） 
7 生産計画は生産実績よりも上振れした値となることが多いため、生産指数全体の計画のバイアスを補正した
試算値（最頻値）が公表されている。

【26年9月景気動向指数】先行CIは低下、一致CIは上昇を見込む 
鉱工業指数の結果を受けて、2026年 11月 9日に公表予定の 9月分の景気動向指数は、先行
CIが前月差▲0.9ptの 117.1、一致 CIが同＋0.7ptの 119.5と予想する（図表 4） 。先行CIで
は構成指標のうち、鉱工業用生産財在庫率指数（逆サイクル）や最終需要財在庫率指数（逆サ
イクル）、東証株価指数などが前月から悪化した。一致 CIでは構成指標のうち、生産指数（鉱
工業）や鉱工業用生産財出荷指数、有効求人倍率（除学卒）などが改善した。この予測値に基
づくと、2026年9月の基調判断は機械的に「改善」に据え置かれる。

図表 4：景気動向指数（先行 CI、一致 CI）と基調判断の推移

Near-term weakness already in the price; maintain Buy and lower TP to NT$286
- Despite near-term margin pressure from softer-than-expected quarterly revenue
and rising depreciation cost, electricity and OPEX, management sees GM
expansion into next year supported by stronger utilization, operating leverage, and
favorable AI-related product mix, in particular substantial upside from wafer

sorting. We lower our earnings projection for 2026/2027 /2028 by 6%/17%/15%
reflecting near-term GM shortfall and potential increasing depreciation cost.
Considering remaining strong AI cycle, we keep our Buy rating but lower our DCF-
derived TP to NT$286 given our lower estimates and concerns on recent
macro/policy uncertainty. Our new TP of NT$286 implies 25x/17x of our 2027 /28
EPS estimates.

### Page 6

King Yuan Electronics Co (2449.TW)
06 November 2026 Citi Research
6
King Yuan Electronics Co
Company description
Founded in 1987, King Yuan Electronics Corp. (KYEC) mainly provides testing
services for the back-end process of semiconductor production globally. With
more than 30 years of industry experience, KYEC has built up firm
relationships with several leading global IC design/IDM customers. Its
headquarters and production facilities are both located in Taiwan, with sales

offices in North America, Japan, and Singapore.
Investment strategy
We rate KYEC as Buy. As a key testing service provider for AI GPU and ASIC
products, KYEC is well positioned to benefit from the significant AI growth
opportunity in the years to come. Currently, KYEC is the major final testing
service provider for its major customer's AI GPU, and we expect KYEC to
continue to secure its dominant share in its customer's upcoming AI GPU
product lines due to its specialty in burn-in testing, smooth product delivery

capability, and its strong business partnership with its customer. We are also
positive over the growth outlook of its smartphone business thanks to the
integration of AI functionality, leading to a better replacement cycle. We
believe KYEC's major customer in smartphone will benefit from an improved
product mix, which will result in longer testing times for the flagship SoC.
Valuation
Our target price for KYEC of NT$286 is derived using a DCF methodology.

Using Bloomberg data, we apply a risk-free rate of 4%, a risk premium of 5%,
and a beta of 1.4x for a 10.8% cost of equity. With 20% corporate tax and a
3.0% interest rate on its debt, our WACC for KYEC is 9.7%. We assume
terminal sales growth of 5%. We model its adjusted EBIT margin to gradually
improve from 23% in 2024 to c30% in 2035E. At our target price, the shares
would trade at 2027 /28E P/B of 4x/4x and 2027 /28E P/E of 25x/17x, in line
with its global peers.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent KYEC shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected end-demand for global
smartphone/consumer electronic products; 2) slower ramp for AI-related
products; 3) slower-than-expected demand recovery in the automotive and
base station sector; and 4) pricing pressure on increasing competition.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

### Page 1

M
Update
King Yuan Electronics Co Ltd  | Asia Pacific
2027 outlook remains
promising; OW
4Q26 guidance cut from the major product delay and weaker smartphone
demand: The company cut 4Q26 revenue guidance  from up >10% to high-single
digit QoQ growth, given the R-series GPU shipment delays and weaker smartphone/
consumer demand. The gross margin should also decline slightly to 36-37%, given
the capacity build but lower-than-expected utilization rate.

1Q27 and 2027 demand remains robust: However, the company reiterated that
delayed demand will be pushed out to 1Q27, suggesting 1Q27 revenue will be up at
least 10-15% QoQ and gross margin will recover to >40%. Looking ahead into 2027,
demand remains robust, and the company expects 2027 revenue to grow 40-50%
YoY with margins >40%.
KYEC in-house burn-in oven could start to support MediaTek TPU: Despite  the
shipment delay from Micro Control Company (MCC), MediaTek management still

raised its 4Q26 TPU revenue projection for both 4Q26 and 2027. We believe it
relates to KYEC's supports of its in-house burn-in oven by end-2026, which could
potentially leave more share to KYEC against ASE if MCC's oven shipments continue
to fall short in  2027.

Key Takeaways
3Q26 results largely in line with both MSe and consensus, but 4Q26 both
revenue and margin guidance missed.

Despite the near-term headwind from the supply chain leading to KYEC's weaker
performance, we expect GPU, TPU, and CPU demand to all ramp up rapidly from
1Q27.
KYEC US capacity is on track for mass production by 4Q27 and full-year
contribution in 2028.
We stay OW on robust 2027 demand, margin recovery, and new capacity
expansion with strong customers' commitment.

n Rise in shipments for core customers such as MediaTek
n Strong AI demand from both GPU and ASIC
n Penetration of wafer sorting business
n Rise in IDM outsourcing to OSAT

Risks to Downside
n Overall non-AI demand remaining weak
n Significant testing market share loss
n Drop in shipments for core customers such as MediaTek

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

- 1 - 
文曄(3036) 
26Q4資料中心恢復成長  
投資評等 目標價 
逢低買進 250 
前次投資建議 2026.8.6： 
「逢低買進」，目標價：$300

報告日期：2026.11.6 研 究 員：林秀泓

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 26Q4 資料中心產品恢復強勁季增，通訊、資料中心產品受益 AI需求。 
➢ 2026 營收翻倍，2027營收增速減緩。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/5 收盤價： 195.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 5,973 億元，QoQ+1.1%，YoY+81.6%，符合
財測 QoQ-3.2%~+3.6%。毛利率 3.60%(QoQ+0.19pt，
YoY-0.14pt)，符合財測 3.5%~3.6%，主要因為產品組合
改善。EPS 7.97元。 
➢ 26Q4 文曄財測以美元匯率 32.2 估計，預估營收
7,300~7,770 億元(QoQ+22.2%~+30.1%)，主要由資料中
心出貨恢復帶動。毛利率 3.2%~3.3%。營業費用
7,154~7,546 億元。財測 EPS 8.07~9.05 元。 
➢ 展望 26Q4 各應用別(以美元估計)，汽車電子受提前拉貨
影響，預期季減雙位數百分比；工業與儀器因季節性因
素，預期季減中個位數百分比；通訊受益於 AI 光通訊需
求，預期季增高雙位數百分比；資料中心與伺服器預期季
增高雙位數百分比，全年成長約兩倍；消費性產品因提前
拉貨，預期季減中雙位數百分比；PC 預期季減；手機旺
季提前拉貨後，預期季減低雙位數百分比。 
➢ 調整預估 2026/2027 年營收 24,151 億元/27,170 億元，

營 運 分 析

文曄為 IC通路代理商 
文曄成立於 1993/12 成立，2002/08 上市掛牌，為提供全球專業電子零組件
通路服務的廠商，透過合併多家 IC通路商的方式持續擴大公司規模。營運總
部設立於台灣，目前在中國、韓國、新加坡、印度、泰國、馬來西亞、越南
等地區共有五十多個營運據點。代理半導體原廠超過 400 家，服務客戶超過
25,000 家。根據 2025 年 Gartner 最新數據，文曄已經成為全球最大和亞太
區最大的半導體通路商，市佔分別為 15.1%和 17.0%。 
產品組合方面，以代理產品的應用劃分營收，26Q3 汽車電子佔 5%、工業與
儀器佔 9%、通訊佔 13%、資料中心及伺服器佔 56%、消費性及其他佔 5%、
個人電腦與週邊佔 4%、手機佔 9%。

26Q3 財報符合財測，獲利再創單季新高 
26Q3 營收 5,973 億元，QoQ+1.1%，YoY+81.6%，符合財測 5,720~6,120
億元(QoQ-3.2%~+3.6%)。毛利率 3.60%(QoQ+0.19pt，YoY-0.14pt)，符合
財測 3.5%~3.6%，主要因為高毛利的工業、車用、Future 營收季增，產品組
合改善。營業費用QoQ+1.6%，費用率1.22%維持低檔，營業槓桿效益持續。
營業利益 142.3 億元，QoQ+9.5%，YoY+151.5%，優於財測中位數 140 億
元。業外方面，淨財務成本約19.4 億元，高於原先預估，因為資金成本上升。
其他業外收入 5.8 億元主要來自權益法認列日電貿的獲利，以及股利收入。
淨利 103.0 億元，QoQ+6.0%，YoY+169.9%，EPS 7.97元。

- 4 - 
26Q4 營收預估季增，資料中心恢復成長 
26Q4 文曄財測以美元匯率 32.2 估計，預估營收 7,300~7,770 億元
(QoQ+22.2%~+30.1%，YoY+113.4%~+127.1%)，主要由資料中心出貨恢復
帶動 。 毛利率3.2%~3.3%。 營業費用7,154~7,546億元 。 財測EPS 8.07~9.05
元。

26Q4 資料中心恢復高雙位數季增，通訊持續成長 
26Q3 分產品線來看(以新台幣計)，汽車電子 QoQ+18%，優於前次法說預期
季減中低個位數百分比；工業與儀器 QoQ+13%，優於預期季增個位數百分
比；通訊 QoQ+5%，符合預期；資料中心與伺服器 QoQ-5%、YoY+114%，
符合預期；消費性產品 QoQ-7%，符合預期；PC週邊 QoQ持平，優於預期
季減個位數百分比，若不含記憶體則QoQ-11%、YoY-20%；手機QoQ+32%，
符合預期，受惠旺季與提前拉貨。 
展望 26Q4 各應用別(以美元估計)，汽車電子：受提前拉貨及庫存調整影響，
預期季減雙位數百分比，YoY+20%以上，全年YoY+30%以上；工業與儀器：
季節性因素，預期季減中個位數百分比，YoY+40%以上，全年成長約40%；
通訊：受益於 AI光通訊需求，預期季增高雙位數百分比，全年成長約一倍；
資料中心與伺服器：出貨節奏調整後恢復，預期季增高雙位數百分比，全年
成長約兩倍，2027 年預期維持高速成長；消費性產品：因提前拉貨，預期季
減中雙位數百分比；PC：預期季減；手機：旺季提前拉貨後，預期季減低雙
位數百分比。

半導體市場成長再度上修 
CSP 的 AI 資本支出展望正向，AI 需求強且具結構性。根據 Gartner 數據，
2026 年全球半導體市場由原先預估 YoY+92%再度上修到 YoY+108%，2027
年預估 YoY+39.2%(不含記憶體+33%)。半導體市場 2025~2029 年 CAGR
由 25.9%上修到 35.1%，2029 年市場規模預估達 2.7 兆美元。data center
為成長最強勁的領域，2026/2027 年預估 YoY+171%/+60%，2025~2029 年
CAGR+54.9%。 
文曄 26Q3 資料中心受出貨節奏影響，26Q4 恢復強勁季增，2026 全年成長
約兩倍，2027 年預期維持高速成長；通訊受益於 AI 光通訊需求，成長動能
延續。Future 方面，歐美市場持續正向復甦，26Q3 營收 YoY+40%以上，車
用與歐洲工廠自動化已無去庫存壓力，資料中心業務也開始接單出貨。Future
較高毛利率可部分抵銷 AI 產品線低毛利率的影響。調整預估 2026/2027 年
營收 24,151 億元/27,170 億元，YoY+105.0%/+12.5%，淨利 387 億元/450
億元，YoY+185.9%/+16.3%，EPS 28.85 元/33.57 元。

目前股價處於 6X 27(F) PER，為過去 PE區間 7~40X的低位。考量 2026 年
AI 帶動營收大幅成長，2027 年 YoY 增速明顯減緩，投資評等維持「逢低買
進」 ，調整目標價 250 元(7X 27(F) PER)，潛在上漲空間 28%。

Document 266: AI“沉船”风险会拖垮美元吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI“沉船”风险会拖垮美元吗？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

巴克莱认为，尽管 AI 相关股票遭遇抛售，但美国经济数据持续强劲、 AI 资本开⽀周期短期
内⼤幅下修的可能性有限，美元情绪指标仍处于正值区间，因此倾向于看好逢低买⼊美
元。
摘要：
AI 投资热潮引发的市场动荡，尚未动摇美元的基本⾯⽀撑。据追⻛交易台消息，巴克莱最新外汇
策略报告指出，美国经济数据持续强劲， AI 资本开⽀周期短期内⼤幅下修的可能性有限，这使得
该⾏倾向于看好在美元回调时逢低买⼊策略。
当前美元⻛险溢价已降⾄约 1% ，为逾⼀年来最低⽔平。分析师指出，欧元兑美元或因欧元空头
仓位拥挤⽽短暂升⾄ 1.14 附近，但鉴于美国宏观基本⾯的持续韧性，这⼀⾛势 “ 难以持续 ” 。
AI 资本开⽀的不确定性，正在以不同⽅式影响各主要货币 —— ⽇元因避险需求受益，欧元则因法
国财政⻛险雪上加霜
从 “ 沉船 ” 说起：历史的镜⼦
巴克莱在报告开篇援引了⼀段历史典故： 1858 年，⼯程师 Isambard Kingdom Brunel 主导建造了
当时世界上最⼤的远洋客轮 “ ⼤东⽅号 ” （ SS Great Eastern ）。这艘船耗尽了委托⽅的资⾦，⼏经

易⼿，最终以废铁价格出售，拆解公司本⾝也陷⼊延误和劳资纠纷。
当然，⼤东⽅号的故事并⾮纯粹的悲剧。这艘船后来被⽤于铺设英美之间的海底电报电缆，催
⽣了⼀项⾜以永久改变世界的技术创新 —— 英镑兑美元汇率的俗称 “cable” ，正是由此⽽来。
该⾏以此类⽐当下的 AI 投资浪潮，指出 “ 这或许是⾃ 1990 年代末以来，第⼀次有⼀个如此规模、
如此雄⼼的项⽬ —— 在商业⽤途、盈利能⼒和影响后果上都是未知之旅 —— 正在我们眼前展
开。⽽且，它正在以⼀种超越我们这⼀⽣对 ' ⻛险承担 ' 感知的⽅式演进。 ”
AI 资本开⽀：⻛险在哪⾥？
该⾏直接点出了这轮 AI 资本开⽀周期中最令⼈担忧的结构性问题。
第⼀，需求的真实性存疑。 当前对算⼒的需求，是否被⼀些中期内难以为继的项⽬推⾄虚⾼？
第⼆，交叉融资的传导⻛险。 芯⽚代⼯⼚、 AI 软件公司与超⼤规模云⼚商之间的延伸交叉融
资，是否在单⼀环节失败时会引发多⽶诺⻣牌效应？
第三，数据中⼼建设的政治阻⼒。 美国中期选举结果出炉后，市场也在追问：数据中⼼建设引

发的反弹，是否会造成延误并推⾼成本？这⼜将如何传导⾄芯⽚制造商的估值？
但报告指出，现有数据中⼼建设合同将按计划推进，未来⼏个季度的建设进度不太可能受到实
质性冲击。从最新财报来看，更充裕的算⼒供给正在推动营收增⻓ —— 这在⼀定程度上必须由
企业端的真实需求驱动，⽽⾮仅靠⼤语⾔模型训练。
超⼤规模云⼚商⽬前没有显⽰出放缓借贷投资计划的迹象，实际上他们预计将在明年底前实现正
向现⾦流。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

更重要的是，巴克莱给出了⼀个宏观层⾯的底线判断：“ 这项新技术已经到来，不会离开。我们
⽆论如何都不可能回到两年前的状态。 ”
美元： AI 抛售尚未形成实质冲击
巴克莱的结论是：⾄少⽬前， AI 相关的股票抛售尚未动摇美元的吸引⼒。
报告指出，现有数据中⼼建设合同将按计划推进，未来⼏个季度的资本开⽀不太可能⼤幅缩
减。超⼤规模云服务商⽬前没有放缓 “ 借钱投资 ” 计划的迹象，并预计将于明年底前实现正向现⾦
流。此外，更⾼的算⼒供给正在推动营收增⻓，这在⼀定程度上反映了企业端的真实需求，⽽
⾮仅仅是⼤型语⾔模型训练所驱动。
从宏观层⾯看，美国经济私⼈国内购买者最终销售增速接近 5% ，经济运⾏在潜在产出⽔平之
上，通胀率仍黏在 3% 附近。该⾏认为， “ 资本开⽀周期将会延续，未来⼀年内⼤幅下修的迹象寥
寥⽆⼏。 ”
当前美元情绪指标仍处于略微正值区间，尚未出现净空头信号。该⾏表⽰，市场可能会出现 “ 美
国即将放缓 ” 的叙事，并推动欧元兑美元短暂升⾄ 1.14 左右，但 “ 鉴于美国宏观背景的持续韧性，

⽆论是当前还是前瞻性来看，这⼀⾛势都难以持续。 ”
欧元：反美元吸引⼒持续消退
欧元作为 “ 反美元 ” 主要替代货币的吸引⼒，正在加速褪⾊。
巴克莱指出，欧元的颓势在美联储 9 ⽉加息后已初现端倪，⽽法国 OAT 利差的急剧扩⼤则进⼀步
加重了压⼒。
在货币政策层⾯， AI 相关股票抛售后，市场对欧洲央⾏的终端利率预期已修正⾄更为现实的约
3% ⽔平。但该⾏认为，欧元的货币政策困境在于：鉴于总需求动能的丧失以及法国债券市场的
脆弱性，进⼀步⼤幅加息⾄ 3% 以上的可能性不⼤。
⽇元：避险需求提供⽀撑
法国的财政问题本⾝已⾜够棘⼿，⽽即将到来的总统⼤选和悬峙议会，使得凝聚共识⼏乎成为不
可能完成的任务。
中期前景依然偏负⾯，⽬前唯⼀的近期亮点是情绪⾯仍偏空，存在⼀定的技术性反弹空间。

### Page 3

AI 投资放缓的担忧，通过股票⻛险溢价的扩⼤，为⽇元带来了避险资⾦流⼊。油价下跌同样在原
则上利好⽇元，尽管这⼀效应可能更多体现在⽇元兑其他货币的交叉盘上，⽽⾮兑美元。
该⾏给出了具体的情景测算：若标普 500 指数从当前约 6% 的跌幅进⼀步扩⼤⾄ 10% （即 2023 年
以来的平均回调幅度），美元兑⽇元可能下⾏约 1.5% ，跌向 155 附近；若跌幅扩⼤⾄ 20% ，⽇元
可能升值 4% ⾄ 5% 。
不过，该⾏也提⽰，⾃夏季协调⼲预以来，投机性仓位已整体转为⽇元净多头，这对⽇元进⼀
步升值的速度构成制约。
亚洲其他货币：科技股波动分化影响
AI 及科技股的动荡，正在亚洲货币市场制造明显分化。
韩元（ KRW ）同样受到科技股波动拖累，本⽉外资股票净流出约 59 亿美元，另有约 20 亿美元的
散⼾净流出。巴克莱认为美元兑韩元近期存在上⾏⻛险， 1325 为⽀撑位。
印度卢⽐（ INR ）则相对受益：油价⼤跌、印度股票指数中 AI 敞⼝较低，叠加外资股票流⼊改善
（⽉内净流⼊约 24 亿美元），为卢⽐提供了有利环境。但巴克莱对印度央⾏（ RBI ）远期账⼾规
模扩⼤、外汇储备下降保持警惕，预计美元兑卢⽐不会⻓期维持在 200 ⽇均线 94.22 下⽅。
⻢来西亚林吉特（ MYR ）则⾯临双重压⼒：作为该地区唯⼀的能源净出⼝国，油价下跌对其相
对更为不利；同时，数据中⼼投资担忧也可能拖累⻢来西亚作为亚洲 AI 及数据中⼼枢纽的吸引
⼒。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Document 272: Anthropic加速推进IPO，计划联手摩根大通CEO直播推介

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic加速推进IPO，计划联手摩根大通CEO直播推介.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-10

### Page 1

Anthropic 计划于本周五安排 CEO 达⾥奥与摩根⼤通 CEO 戴蒙进⾏直播推介，周⼆已启动
路演，冲刺感恩节前登录纳斯达克。公司拟参考 SpaceX 模式筹资超 800 亿美元，由⾼盛领
衔主承销，摩根⼤通负责散⼾发售。近期市场估值预期已⾃近 2 万亿降⾄ 1.1 万亿⾄ 1.3 万亿
美元。
摘要：
Anthropic 正加快推进潜在的创纪录 IPO 。为给上市造势，公司计划安排联合创始⼈兼 CEO 达⾥
奥 · 阿莫迪（ Dario Amodei ）与摩根⼤通董事⻓兼 CEO 杰⽶ · 戴蒙（ Jamie Dimon ）进⾏⼀场直
播对谈，向潜在投资者推介公司及其 AI 业务前景。
据彭博援引知情⼈⼠报道，这场直播预计将在本周五纽约午餐时间前后举⾏。 Anthropic 本周⼆
启动正式路演，并争取在美国感恩节假期前完成定价、登陆纳斯达克。
不过，在近期 AI 板块估值回调的背景下， Anthropic 的定价预期已明显降温。市场⼈⼠⽬前讨论
的估值区间约为1.1 万亿⾄ 1.3 万亿美元，较此前⼀度接近 2 万亿美元的预期⼤幅回落。

直播推介参考 SpaceX 模式
知情⼈⼠称，阿莫迪与戴蒙的直播对谈，在形式上参考了 SpaceX 今年 6 ⽉ IPO 期间安排⻢斯克出
席推介活动的做法，但整体规格将相对低调。
SpaceX 当时通过 IPO 及超额配售合计募资 862 亿美元，创下美股 IPO 募资纪录。 Anthropic 此次
计划募集的资⾦规模据称可能与之相当，甚⾄更⾼。
对于相关安排， Anthropic 和摩根⼤通均拒绝置评。
此次由戴蒙参与直播，也凸显了摩根⼤通与 Anthropic 之间⽇益密切的合作关系。本周⼀，
Anthropic 曾在官⽹披露，摩根⼤通正将旗下编程⼯具 Claude Code 从软件⼯程团队推⼴⾄更多
业务部⻔。
⾼盛主承销，摩根⼤通主攻散⼾发⾏
从承销架构看，⾼盛获得了最关键的左侧主承销商席位，摩根⼠丹利则担任稳定代理商，相关
信息已体现在 Anthropic 提交给美国证券交易委员会的⽂件中。
摩根⼤通是参与此次交易的 22 家银⾏之⼀，主要负责美国散⼾投资者的营销和股份配售⼯作。

此前有报道称， Anthropic 正考虑将约 15% ⾄ 20% 的 IPO 股份分配给散⼾投资者。
花旗集团则负责协调国际零售发⾏，覆盖英国、欧洲部分地区、加拿⼤、⽇本和澳⼤利亚等市
场。
在⼤型科技公司 IPO 中，提⾼散⼾配售⽐例有助于扩⼤市场参与度和交易热度，但也会对上市后
的股价表现带来更⾼要求。尤其是在当前市场波动加剧的背景下， Anthropic 能否平衡融资规
模、估值⽔平与⼆级市场表现，成为投资者关注的重点。
AI 估值回调，机构要求 “ 留出安全边际 ”
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

图⽚
Anthropic 的上市定价正⾯临来⾃市场的压⼒。
据 华 尔 街 ⻅ 闻 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇ ⽂ 章 ， 在 纳 斯 达 克 100 指 数 单 ⽇ 下 跌 6.4% 后 ， 部 分 潜 在 投 资 者 将
Anthropic 的合理估值区间下调⾄ 1.2 万亿⾄ 1.3 万亿美元。相⽐之下，在 10 ⽉公司财务简报后，
投资者最初给出的反馈约为 1.4 万亿⾄ 1.5 万亿美元；⽽在 10 ⽉初，市场对其估值的最⾼预期⼀度
接近 2 万亿美元。⽬前，相关讨论仍在进⾏，最终定价尚未确定。
多位机构⼈⼠认为， Anthropic 需要在估值上作出⾜够让步，才能吸引⻓期资⾦并保证上市后的
交易表现。
Gallo Partners ⾸席投资官 Michael Alfaro 表⽰， Anthropic 若想建⽴ “ ⾼质量的订单簿 ” ，估值应控

制在 1.3 万亿⾄ 1.4 万亿美元之间。 Sycomore Asset Management 科技组合经理 David Rainville
则认为，若定价接近 1.2 万亿美元，吸引投资者 “ 不会有太⼤困难 ” 。
汇丰⾸席多资产策略师 Max Kettner 的态度更为谨慎。他认为， Anthropic 的估值应低于 1 万亿美
元，原因并不只是发⾏能否完成，更在于上市后的股价能否站稳。
“ 更重要的是⼆级市场表现， ”Kettner 表⽰， “ 如果出现类似 SpaceX 的情况 —— 投资者在⼆级市场
买⼊后，股价短暂冲⾼、随后迅速回落 —— 这会是当前市场环境中最不希望看到的结果。 ”
对 Anthropic ⽽⾔，这场由阿莫迪和戴蒙参与的直播，或许能够强化公司在技术实⼒、商业化进
展和⾦融机构合作上的叙事；但最终决定 IPO 成败的，仍将是估值是否⾜够贴近市场的⻛险偏
好。

### Page 1

for a changing

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by large market swings triggered by
the US midterms results. In the end, energy prices end the week much
lower, bond yields somewhat lower and stock prices down in tech-heavy
markets and slightly up elsewhere, notably in Europe. Incoming data
confirmed a picture of resilient growth overall. The Bank of England

hiked its policy rate by 25 bps and signaled it could do more. Market
pressure on France eased somewhat.
EUROZONE
Energy pushes inflation up, but underlying pressures remain rela -
tively contained. Headline inflation rose to 4.0% y/y in October, its
highest in three years, strengthening the case for another ECB rate
increase in December. Energy remained the main driver, while core in-
flation rose by 2.6%. The ECB wage tracker points to negotiated wage

growth slowing to 2.3% by September 2027. Meanwhile, retail sales fell
0.7% m/m in September and the average interest rate on new business
loans increased 13bp to 3.90%.
To watch next week: September trade balance, Q3 GDP second esti -
mate and employment (Friday).
France
EVs boost activity, market pressure eases somewhat. After already
bolstering household consumption in Q3, new car registrations rose
7% y/y in October (+4% y/y YTD), driven by another surge in electric

car sales (+81% y/y in October; +78% y/y YTD). In parallel, manufac -
turing output rebounded by 0.5% m/m in September, supported by
the automotive sector (+7.5% m/m but -5.5% y/y) and capital goods
(+2.6% m/m and +4.8% y/y). However, industrial production fell by
0.5% m/m (and 1.3% y/y), as a result of a decline in electricity produc-
tion (-4.2% m/m). The composite October PMI was revised down by
1.8 points from the flash estimate to 46.6 (-4.5 m/m), dragged down by

services. This sharp downgrade likely reflects the hit to sentiment from
the October 17 riots; however, precedents suggest activity itself should
not be visibly impacted. Appropriately indifferent to the meandering
journey of the draft budget through Parliament, the OAT-bund spread
narrowed to 155 bps (-10 bps in one week) amid a broad-based decline
in government bond yields across advanced economies; Thursday’s
long-term debt auction, the last of the year, was well-subscribed. Stri-

ving to meet its deficit target of 5.4% of GDP in 2026, the government
announced EUR3.8bn of spending cuts to offset EUR3.7bn in additional
expenditure. The High Council for Public Finance nevertheless sees the
deficit reaching 5.5%. The 2027 budget will start being discussed by
the Senate from next week, in its original version, after the national
Assembly unanimously rejected its amended version that would have
led to a 5.9% deficit.
To watch next week: Q3 unemployment rate and the Banque de France

monthly business survey (Tuesday).
Germany
Industrial activity picks up. New orders rebounded 4.4% m/m in Sep -
tember and industrial production increased 0.7%, with the auto sec -
tor delivering particularly striking gains of 10.5% in orders and 17.1%
in production. Mechanical-engineering orders also recovered (+8.3%),
while pharmaceutical production increased by 8.0%. By contrast, large
orders in aerospace, shipbuilding, rail and defence fell by 10.4%, while

machinery and equipment production declined by 6.5%. October's com-
posite PMI was revised up to 53.4, signaling continued strong expan -
sion.
Italy
Services keep the economy above water as manufacturing slips back.
The composite PMI eased to 50.8 in October, its lowest since May.
Manufacturing returned to contraction at 49.4, its weakest reading
since January, while services strengthened to 52.0 and recorded a fifth
consecutive month of growth. Both sectors reported increasing input-

cost and output-price inflation.
UNITED KINGDOM
The BoE hikes and leaves the door open to more. The Bank of England
raised Bank Rate by 25bp to 4%, its first increase since August 2023, in a
more hawkish than expected 8-1 vote, with Catherine Mann favouring
a 50bp hike. The November Monetary Policy Report sees CPI inflation
peaking around 5% y/y by mid-2027 and upgraded Q4 2026 growth to
2.2%, while cutting the 2027 and 2028 growth projections. The com -

posite PMI meanwhile improved to 52.9, its highest since February,
with new business growing at its strongest pace in nine months and
private-sector employment increasing for the first time in 25 months.
To watch next week: Q3 GDP , business investment, industrial produc-
tion (Thursday).
UNITED STATES
Post election market jitters and a hot jobs report. The Democrats’
unexpectedly sweeping victory in the midterms (the largest wave
since 1974) initially led markets to rally on (i) relief that the President

recognised the results, and (ii) hopes of a swift end to the Iran war
(oil prices and bond yields down, stock prices up). But this was soon
followed by a sharp equity sell-off on 5 November, with the S&P 500
down 4.4% and the Nasdaq down 5.0%, erasing USD2.7 Trillion of mar-
ket capitalization in one day. Why? Because of the realization that vo -
ters strongly supported AI-restrictive measures on the ballot (46 out
of 47) and candidates — for Congress and Governor seats — in favour

of curtailing data centers buildout. By the week-end, however, senti -
ment had turned around again, helped in part by a much stronger than
expected jobs report: payrolls increased by 165,000 in October against
a 70,000 consensus, while the previous two months were revised hi -
gher and unemployment edged down to 4.1%. Average hourly earnings
rose 0.4% m/m, their strongest monthly pace since November 2025.
Manufacturing activity also strengthened, with the ISM rising to 55.5,

and the non-manufacturing index easing to a still buoyant 54.5.
To watch next week: October CPI (Tuesday); October PPI (Friday).
JAPAN
Wage growth accelerates. Scheduled contractual earnings increased
3.7% y/y in September and total wage growth accelerated to 4.6%,
helped by strong bonus payments. Real wage growth remained posi -
tive at 2.2% y/y.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Portfolio outflows. According to IIF data, non-resident portfolio invest-
ment flows recorded USD33.8bn of outflows in October, the largest

monthly outflow since March and up from USD19.3bn in September.
Equities accounted for USD31.7bn, led by USD28.3bn from emerging
Asia, as doubts over AI-investment returns and profit-taking hit North
Asian markets particularly hard. Taiwan and South Korea suffered a
further sharp market correction in the first week of November.

### Page 2

7
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ASIA
PMI: expanding except for the Philippines. The ASEAN PMI rose to 53.1
in October from 52.1, pointing to faster expansion. India remained ro -
bust despite a slight decline (54.7). In Taiwan and South Korea, the
manufacturing PMIs declined but remained expansionary, at 54.9 and
51.3, respectively. Indonesia’s real GDP growth accelerated to 5.5% y/y

in Q3 2026 from 5.3% in Q2 marking its fastest pace since 2013.
China
The PBOC steps in as equity correction deepens. The central bank in -
creased its use of liquidity tools designed to support equity purchases,
including the special relending facility for share buybacks and stake in-
creases and a third round of the Securities, Funds and Insurance Com-
panies Swap Facility, for which 31 institutions applied for more than
RMB100bn. The measures follow heavy stock-market losses between

June and October. The PBOC also raised the reserve-requirement ratio
on foreign-currency deposits to 6% from 4%, effective 15 November, in
an effort to slow the renminbi's appreciation.
Weak domestic inflation pressure: In October, CPI inflation slowed to
0.7% y/y from 0.9% in September. Fuel prices increased by 20.2% y/y,
food prices fell -2.9% and core inflation slowed to 0.8% y/y from 1.0%.
Producer price inflation continued to rise (reaching 5.7% vs. 5.0% in

September), principally driven by higher energy and electronics prices.
To watch next week: External-trade data (Tuesday).
EMEA
Central Europe
PMIs expansionary except in Romania. Poland’s PMI hit the 50-mark
for the first time since April 2025 thanks to export orders. PMI was
also higher in Czech Rep. (55), supported by new orders, output and
employment. Hungary’s PMI fell marginally to 53.1.
Poland and Czech Republic central banks hold and hike respectively,

as expected.
Gulf
PMIs resilient except Qatar (deteriorating further to 46.3).
Saudi Arabia: Massive budget deficit in sight. Saudi Arabia recorded a
budget deficit of USD22.5bn in Q3, narrowing by 5% y/y. But over the
first nine months of 2026, budget deficit reached USD65.1bn, up from
USD48bn in 2025, despite a rather good revenue performance (+2.8%
total; +7.8% for non-oil revenues; -1.3% for oil revenues). The 8% rise in
spending in Q1-Q3 partly due to the war will make it difficult to meet

the 2026 revised target (deficit of 4.9% of GDP).
LATIN AMERICA
PMIs remain weak across the region, with only Colombia in expansion.
Brazil’s central bank pauses rate cuts, whereas Mexico’s central bank
cuts rates whilst signalling an upcoming pause. On 4 November, Bra -
zil’s central bank held its Selic rate at 13.75%, interrupting a series of
five consecutive cuts, amid rising inflation (4.6% y/y in September, up
from 4.2%), a sharp post-election real depreciation on fiscal concerns,

and higher energy prices. The central bank also revised up its 2027
inflation forecast to 4.7% (from 3.9%) and removed forward guidance
hinting at further cuts. Mexico’s central bank unexpectedly cut its po -
licy rate by 25bp to 6.25% on 5 November. This decision came despite
above-target inflation (3.5% against a 3% target), a peso depreciation
of more than 9% against USD since September, and relatively strong Q3
GDP growth (3.2% y/y). The central bank signaled that rates would now

remain on hold for an extended period.
COMMODITIES
Geopolitical relief delivers a dramatic energy-price reversal. Mar -
kets have priced in the reduced risk of short-term conflict escalation
through a sharp decline in prices. Beyond this, however, they lack di -
rection, wavering between positive signals (the resumption of indirect
negotiations between the belligerents) and less favorable ones (in -
creased vessel damage in the strait). Brent dropped 7.6% over the week

to USD96.8/bbl as markets priced a reduced risk of near-term conflict
escalation. European gas prices fell almost in lockstep, declining 7.7%
to EUR87.6/MWh. US commercial crude inventories continued to de -
cline, although more modestly than over the preceding four weeks.
Diesel crack spread’s reaction to US elections has been much more
limited than for crude. EU gas storage ended the refilling season at 75%
of capacity (vs. 80% goal).
y/y = year-on-year

m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels
MBtu = Million British Thermal Units

Document 340: Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer
Date:    6 hours ago
Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 4 Nov 2026
View in browser

I. Why Wall Street Is Cheering the Blue Sweep
Ahead of Tuesday's midterm elections, we warned that a Democratic
sweep of Congress could deliver a cold shower to Wall Street. It still could,
but it didn't do so today. Democrats captured both chambers of Congress,
flipping seven Republican-held Senate seats, including historic victories in
Texas, Kansas, and South Carolina. Financial markets put a positive spin
on the blue wave.
Oil prices fell on hopes that the US and Iran are more likely now to resolve
their war diplomatically. If so, then the outlook for inflation will improve. So
the Fed might not have to raise the federal funds rate as much. The

### Page 2

federal deficit might not widen further if Democrats refuse to increase
defense spending, while President Donald Trump vetoes their attempts to
provide Medicare for all. If Democrats slow the AI buildout, hyperscalers'
profits and free cash flow will increase. Fewer AI-related bond issues
would also help keep a lid on bond yields.
Initially, Nasdaq-100 futures plunged as much as 2.4% overnight, while
S&P 500 futures fell 1.4%. But the selloff reversed after Trump struck a

surprisingly conciliatory tone, congratulating Democrats and saying he
was willing to work with them.
That is when the cheer on Wall Street started. The Dow surged 3.0%
today, the strongest post-midterm rally since 1982. The S&P 500 jumped
1.6%, its best session in three months. The Nasdaq-100 advanced 0.9%.
The relief rally extended beyond equities. The 10-year Treasury yield fell
to 4.90%, back in what we have called the "old normal" range of
4.00%-5.00% (chart).

The dollar index (DXY) fell more than 1.5%, its biggest decline since
September. Gold rallied back above $4,000 an ounce. It might be finally
bottoming.

### Page 3

de-escalation in the Middle East
Brent crude plunged 8.7% to $94.62 a barrel amid hopes of Middle East
de-escalation. The nearby futures price of gasoline plunged to $2.73
(chart).
Investors may be warming up to the idea that some constraints on the AI
buildout could be healthy, as we have been arguing recently. Democratic
victories in key data center states, including Texas and Georgia, raise the
prospect of tighter construction restrictions amid concerns about electricity

and water consumption.
Hyperscalers rallied today, with Amazon and Microsoft gaining more than
6%, as capital discipline could ease concerns about AI investment returns
and improve free cash flow (chart). AI infrastructure providers struggled,

### Page 4

however. The PHLX Semiconductor Index fell 3.1% amid fears of weaker
demand from a slower buildout. We may be entering an AI Goldilocks
zone, with enough restraint to take some air out of the bubble without
derailing the boom.
II. The US Economy Continues To Perform Well
The economy and, therefore, earnings remain fundamentally resilient,
which continues to support the bull market. With the midterms behind us
and political uncertainty receding, investors can refocus on these strong

fundamentals. Here are the highlights of today's upbeat economic
indicators:
(1) ISM Services PMI. The ISM-NM PMI edged down modestly to 54.5 in
October, marking its 28th consecutive month of expansion (chart).
Underlying demand remained solid, with new orders and business activity
both comfortably above the 50.0 expansion threshold. Encouragingly, the
employment index rose from 50.1 to 51.4, marking its second consecutive
month of expansion.

### Page 5

Inflation is still a concern. The ISM prices-paid indexes remained elevated
in October (chart).
(2) Labor market. The average of the ISM employment indexes rose to
51.9 in October, its highest reading in roughly three years (chart). ADP
reported an 86,000 increase in private payrolls in October, beating
consensus expectations of 67,000.

### Page 6

(3) Trade balance. The latest trade data point to resilient US and global
economic growth. Merchandise imports rose 2.4% m/m in September to a
record $3.44 trillion annualized, while exports increased 2.5% m/m to
$2.09 trillion. The merchandise trade deficit widened to $1.35 trillion
annualized (chart).
Nominal and real US merchandise exports plus imports rose 21.1% y/y
and 10.1%, respectively, in September (chart). That suggests global trade
remains firmly in expansion territory and is consistent with strong global

economic growth.

### Page 7

Selected technology imports reached a record $61.3 billion in September,
reflecting the AI buildout’s heavy reliance on imported semiconductors,
servers, and other hardware (chart).

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH FROM LSEG 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS

This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate  
earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 30, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.4%. 
 Of the 299 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 81.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 13.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 12.2%. 
 69.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 19.0. 
 During the week of Nov. 2, 144 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings. 
SECTION A: EARNINGS OUTLOOK 
Q3 2026: EARNINGS REVISIONS 
EXHIBIT 1A.  S&P 500: Q3 2026 SHARE-WEIGHTED EARNINGS ($B)

Source: LSEG I/B/E/S

### Page 2

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH FROM LSEG OCTOBER 30, 2026 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.  2 
There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $778.4B 
from $760.7B). Three of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-20.5%) and materials (-7%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (33%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. 
Overall, share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 2.3% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-14.7B) and materials (-1.2B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (15.7B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 17.7B since the start of the quarter.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 31.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.4%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $778.4B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer 
Discretionary sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (146.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $63B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.6B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (566.5%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (143.8%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (68.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $260.2B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $154B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (137%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (71.5%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 28.2%. 
The Consumer Discretionary sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn 
$56.3B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $54.2B in 25Q3. 7 of the 18 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to 
see lower earnings than a year ago. The Casinos & Gaming (-44.1%) and Automotive Parts & Equipment (-38.8%) 
sub-industries have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate 
improves to 4.5%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 147 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, 17 positive

surprises and 11 negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

Editorial 
Opportunities from the bond sell-off  
Strategy summary: The global bond sell-off is creating tactical 
and medium-term opportunities. In the US, September’s yield 
surge has improved the risk -reward in 10 -year government 
bonds, presenting an attractive entry point for investors with a 
6-12-month horizon. We believe bond and money markets are 
overly hawkish about the Fed outlook, given disinflation is likely 
to return as impact of oil prices and tariffs ease next year. 
In equities, we stick to quality, especially sectors riding the AI -
led earnings boom. The upcoming Q3 earnings season is likely 
to show AI -investments are powering sectors beyond the 
technology and communications services, such as financials, 
materials and power and electrification. We remain constructive 
on the semiconductor and memory segments. We also expect 
the US Dollar to peak in the next few weeks along with short -
to-medium-term US bond yields. This creates opportunities for 
AUD corporate bonds, Emerging Market USD bonds and gold. 
Attractive risk -reward in US 10 -year government bonds.  
The US 10-year bond yield rose more than 50bps in September 
(biggest monthly rise since 2022) and 115bps this year, amid 
expectations of economic resilience, higher Fed rates, fuel -
driven inflation, AI-related capital demand and fiscal concerns. 
Valuations appear close to fair value, improving the 6-12-month 
risk-reward. Bond arithmetic suggests losses of less than 1% if 
the yield rises to 6%, vs. more than 10% gains if it falls to 4.5%. 
Resilient US economic activity, but softer -than-expected 
inflation. September data showed robust US employment 
(jobless claims, ADP) and core capital-goods orders. However, 
consumer confidence remains depressed, real disposable 
income is flat and households are drawing on savings to sustain 
spending. Against this divided backdrop, core PCE inflation - 
the Fed’s preferred gauge - was softer than expected at 0.2% 
m/m and 3.0% y/y. A revised method to measure PCE inflation 
also indicate that annual core PCE inflation was 35bps lower 
than previously reported, narrowing the gap with CPI inflation. 
Markets appear overly hawkish on the Fed. Money markets 
price about 85bps of tightening over the next 12 months. A 
strong labour market (September’s non -farm payrolls data is 
due tonight) may justify one or two further hikes, but aggressive 
tightening after the sharp rise in bond yields – which has 
already tightened financial conditions - could threaten financial 
stability. US small- and mid-cap equities have already fallen 8–
9% from recent peaks over the past couple of months, 
materially underperforming large caps, as energy and 
borrowing-cost concerns intensify. W hile some Fed speakers 
remain hawkish, we  agree with New York Fed President 
Williams’ that there is no urgency to tighten. We see little 
chance of an October Fed rate hike and expect inflation data 
for Sep-Nov determining whether the Fed moves in December. 
Stay with quality equities and AI beneficiaries.  US large 
caps have absorbed the rise in bond yields relatively well, 
supported by expectations of robust earnings growth led by AI-
related investment. We continue to favour quality companies 
and sectors benefiting from this AI cycle. Semiconductors and 
memory remain preferred exposures: structural AI demand 
should extend the memory upcycle, while long -term supply 
agreements provide better revenue visibility. The opportunity is 
also broadening beyond technology and communication 
services into financials, materials, power and electrification. 
Opportunities in AUD corporate bonds and gold.  The USD 
rebound alongside higher US yields offers a tactical opportunity 
to add higher -yielding Emerging Market USD and non -USD 
bonds. We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds, which 
offer a yield premium over comparable US credit. Our 
constructive medium-term AUD/USD view could add returns for 
USD-based investors over the next 6 –12 months. We would 
also use gold’s recent pullback to add exposure, supported by 
sustained Emerging Market central bank demand. We continue 
to view USD 4,000/oz as important technical support for gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Top client questions 
  Do you expect more RBA rate hikes after its less hawkish 
guidance? What is your view on the AUD and AUD bonds? 
Our view: We expect the RBA to leave rates unchanged, but 
retain the option to hike again should inflation intensify. We 
maintain our medium-term constructive AUD view and maintain 
our opportunistically bullish AUD corporate bonds idea. 
Rationale: The RBA’s forward guidance remains data dependent, 
explicitly allowing further tightening if needed as inflation remains too 
high.  At 4.60%, Australia’s cash rate is currently 60–85bp above the 
Fed’s 3.75–4.00% range. Thus, even with our two additional 25bp s 
Fed hikes expectation, Australian rates would hold a modest positive 
rate differential. We expect the AUD to strengthen once the Fed 
signals that US rates are near their peak, which should allow resilient 
Australian fundamentals to reassert themselves. Key risks are 
persistently high US yields, weaker China growth or a sharper 
deterioration in global risk sentiment. 
We believe risk-reward has become more attractive for AUD bonds 
following the bear -flattening of the AUD yield curve. Markets are 
already pricing in one more RBA hike through end-2027, presenting 
an attractive opportunity to earn a yield even as increasingly 
restrictive financial conditions reinforc e disinflation over time. 
Meanwhile, the positive interest rate differential with the US supports 
the currency outlook, supporting the case for AUD bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  What does the incoming US data tell us about the path for 
US 10-year bond yields from here? 
Our view: The risk -reward in US 10 -year yields has turned 
attractive following their move higher amid incoming US data. 
Rationale: Recent US data has indeed started to show softer-than-
expected inflation. However, market technicals are likely temporarily 
delaying a move lower in bond yields, with reports suggesting the  
rise in yields is creating forced selling amongst hedge funds and real 
estate investment trusts. Nevertheless, we do not expect this to be 
an extended selloff. We have initiated an opportunistic bullish idea 
on US 10-year government bonds this week and continue to believe 
this idea offers an attractive risk/reward on a 6-12-month horizon as 
yields become attractive enough to entice buyer flow to return. On a 
broader allocation basis, we retain our structural preference for 3-7-
year maturities in bonds. 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds amid 
Australia-US interest rate differentials and positive 
outlook for the AUD

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in the GBP ahead of the UK 
budget announcement next month? 
Our view: We maintain a medium-term constructive GBP view, 
but keep near-term exposure measured ahead of the 28 October 
Budget. We would view any pre -Budget weakness as an 
opportunity to gradually build GBP exposure. 
Rationale: The government’s decision to adjust the pension triple 
lock from 2030 is an early indication of greater willingness to tackle 
longer-term spending pressures, reinforcing Chancellor Healey’s 
commitment to fiscal discipline and meeting the fiscal rules. Markets 
are likely to scrutinise the Budget closely for evidence that this 
discipline extends to near -term tax and spending decisions. A 
credible Budget should help contain pressure on UK government 
bond yields and support GBP. Any fiscal slippage remains a key risk.  
Near term, we prefer measured GBP exposure as elevated US yields 
and repricing towards further Fed tightening could continue to 
support the USD. Over the medium term, we expect GBP to rebound 
as the Fed eventually signals the peak of its tightening cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  How would different US mid-term election outcome 
scenarios affect the outlook for US equities? 
Our view: We believe election outcomes will have a limited 
impact over the next 12 months. We remain positive on US 
equities driven by fundamental earnings growth.  
Rationale: Over the past 50 years, the ruling US President’s party 
has retained control of both chambers of Congress on only two 
occasions (1978 and 2002). All other elections result ed in an 
opposition sweep or a divided government.  
Based on the latest prediction markets, it appears that an opposition 
sweep (both the House and Senate flips from Republican to 
Democrats) is more likely than a divided government ( in which the 
House flips to Democrats , while the Senate remains under 
Republican control). A scenario where Republicans retain a majority 
is least likely per prediction markets. 
While mid -term elections may introduce near -term volatility, we 
caution against overreaction. Historically, the average return for the 
S&P 500 following an opposition sweep is 17% over the next 12 
months, but the average return following a divided government is not 
significantly worse, at 15%. We prefer to base our investment views 
on fundamental earnings growth, where investor attention should 
focus on the upcoming Q3 earnings season. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the outlook for  the memory chip and broader 
semiconductors industry? 
Our view: We remain positive on semiconductors and memory 
chips. Structural AI demand should prolong the memory 
upcycle while long-term agreements improve revenue visibility. 
Rationale: Recent industry commentary points to tighter memory 
supply-demand conditions through 2028, reinforcing our view that AI 
demand is likely to extend the memory upcycle. Furthermore, adoption 
of long-term agreements improves revenue and earnings visibility. 
Despite debates around “Pacing the Frontier”, we have seen a flurry 
of new model releases, along with the introduction of new consumer 
AI agents capable of comparing prices online and making 
purchases. These are early days, but the path is open for a business-
to-agent (B2A) economy where businesses increasingly interact with 
AI assistants acting on customers’ behalf. As these applications are 
increasingly inference intensive, wider adoption should support 
demand for memory, with its share of global semiconductor 
revenues projected to rise from 30.7% in 2025 to 66.7% in 2027. 
Against this backdrop, we maintain our Opportunistic idea on Global 
Semiconductors. Given memory  chips’ cyclical nature, investors 
should avoid overconcentration in memory names and maintain a 
diversified exposure across Big Tech and semiconductors. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Does a US bond yield stress test raise the risk to your 
constructive view on US equities? 
Our view: We have a target for the S&P 500 index of 8400 over 
the next 12 months. A scenario of a sustained rise in the US 10-
year government bond yield to 5.6% could lead to negative 
returns from the current S&P500 level (7,666). 
Rationale: Our base case 12-month target for the S&P 500 is 8,400, 
which assumes 2027 S&P 500 EPS of USD 420 and a 20x forward 
P/E multiple. A rise in bond yields would have an impact on equity 
valuations, as a higher discount rate reduces the present value of 
future earnings. W e stress -test our target under different 10 -year 
yield and earnings scenarios.  
A rise in bond yields would arguably be associated with a stronger 
growth environment and hence, even higher earnings. However, 
assuming unchanged EPS at our base case of USD 420, a 
(sustained) rise in the 10 -year bond yield to 5.6% would lead to 
negative returns from the current level  if not offset by a further 
improvement in earnings growth . However, in o ur base case  we 
expect the 10-year yield is more likely to fall from here. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

The memory segment is projected to account for 
two-thirds of global semiconductor revenues by

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand  has even widened this year . Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months . Retail sales have barely 
increased, the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting 
economic growth are expected to increase in the second half of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.0% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 2.8% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.4% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would ease slightly to 1.2% in 2026 (1.3% in 2025), before edging back to 1.3% in 2027. The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank Negara Malaysia (BNM)  (5 
November)

As written in our Macro Strategy note , 
BNM’s final meeting of 2026 on 5 
November will be live and a close call, 
after the central bank opened the door to 
monetary policy normalisation during its 
September review. BNM Governor Abdul 
Rasheed Ghaffour reiterated in a 
Bloomberg interview on 15 Oc tober that 
the central bank has space to adjust 
policy if needed, as policymakers are 
vigilant about upside inflationary 
pressures amid strong economic growth. 
He also said that policy decisions will be 
data dependent. We think the latest 
available Septemb er data for the 
upcoming meeting suggest that 
policymakers can afford to be patient 
until 1H27, maintaining the Overnight 
Policy Rate at 2.75% for now. Inflation 
remained contained within the lower half 
of the official 2026 inflation forecast 
range of 1.5 -2.5%, notwithstanding 
external price pressures and robust 
economic activity. Advance estimates 
showed GDP growth of 5.3% yoy in 3Q26, 
while exports jumped to a five -year high 
of 54.5% yoy in September, 
supercharged by the artificial 
intelligence boom. However, headline 
inflation eased slightly to 1.8% yoy in 
September 2026 from 1.9% yoy in August, 
while core inflation remained stable at 1.7% 
yoy. Fuel subsidies have limited the pass-
through of upstream cost pressures to 
consumer prices, alongside stable 
domestic demand conditions.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

South Korea

October trade and inflation data are due 
for release. Export growth is expected to 
accelerate to nearly 100% yoy, up from 
83.7% in September, while the trade 
surplus is forecast to remain above 
USD40bn for another month. CPI 
inflation is expected to hold steady at 2.9% 
yoy. Overall, the data should reinforce 
the view that AI - and semiconductor -
driven growth and the expansion in 
South Korea’s trade surplus remain 
firmly on track, providing fundamental 
support for the KOSPI and KRW. 
Meanwhile, inflation remains sticky 
above the BOK’s 2% target, bolstering 
the case for further monetary tightening 
as early as the November policy meeting.

Taiwan

October inflation data are forthcoming. 
Headline CPI inflation is expected to 
remain above 2% for the sixth 
consecutive month, at 2.3% yoy. Core 
CPI inflation is also likely to stay above 2% 
for the sixth consecutive month, at 2.3% 
yoy. Despite government subsidies on 
fuel and electricity prices, higher global 
energy prices continue to feed through 
to CPI via items such as airfares and tour 
package fees. Services inflation also 
remains sticky, particularly in food away 
from home, housin g, transportation, and 
entertainment. Meanwhile, domestic 
inflation expectations appear to be 
gradually rising. A recent private -sector 
survey showed that the public expects

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

inflation to average 2.5% in 2026, with 
52% of respondents expecting inflation 
to exceed 2.5%. Against this backdrop, 
we maintain our view that the central 
bank will resume rate hikes in December, 
raising the policy rate by 12.5  bps to 
2.125%.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to rise to 3.2% yoy  in October 2026, 
extending the increase over the previous 
two months, and moving slightly above 
the upper end of the Bank of Thailand’s 
1-3% target range. This was likely driven 
by faster energy inflation amid an 
uptrend in diesel prices, alongside a 
rebound in food inflation. The inflation 
uptrend was likely broadly in line with 
the central bank's expectations and 
mainly supply-side driven, supporting its 
decision to look through the temporary 
price pressures and keep its policy rate 
unchanged at its October meeting.

Vietnam

We expect Vietnam’s October 2026 
goods exports and retail sales to remain 
robust at 42.0% yoy and 14.0% yoy year-
to-date, respectively, while headline 
inflation is likely to stay elevated at 
around 5.0% yoy. Exports have 
continued to be supported by strong  
electronics and machinery shipments 
amid a favourable technology cycle with 
spillovers from robust artificial 
intelligence-related demand. Retail 
sales were likely bolstered by firm 
household spending, supported by 
favourable labour market conditions, 
and continued strength in tourism. 
Headline inflation has remained elevated 
and above the central bank’s target of 
4.5% for the year, driven by price 
pressures from transport and energy, 
food, as well as material costs. This 
poses a challenge for policymakers as 
they seek to balance inflati on risks 
against ambitions of achieving at least 10% 
economic growth this year.

Hong Kong

Hong Kong’s economy is expected to 
decelerate from 4.3% yoy in Q2 to 2.6% 
in Q3, largely due to slower private 
consumption. Despite a 13% increase in 
Mainland visitor arrivals during the 
period, domestic consumption continued 
to be weighed down by a weaken ing 
labour market, with the unemployment 
rate elevated at 3.9% in September. 
Meanwhile, higher interest rates have 
dampened investment sentiment and 
capital market activity. The Centa -City 
Leading Index remained broadly flat, 
while the Hang Seng Index was range-
bound at around 25,000. The positive 
wealth effect seen in 1H has faded as 
asset market performance remained 
muted. A strong HKD continued to 
encourage outbound tourism, evidenced 
by an increase in average daily resident 
departures from 6.9% yoy in Q2 to 10.9% 
in Q3.

However, GDP growth is expected to be 
buffered by improving net exports amid 
the ongoing AI -electronics upcycle. 
Export growth picked up and outpaced 
import growth during Q3. Hong Kong, as 
China’s free port and re -export hub, is 
benefiting from stronger de mand for 
Chinese electronic goods.

Indonesia/ Philippines

### Page 4

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Indonesia’s 3Q GDP likely moderated to 
5.2% y/y. Soft consumer confidence and 
retail sales growth point to weaker 
consumption, compounded by a gradual 
rise in inflation. Fiscal measures likely 
helped spending among low -income 
households. Despite the quarte rly 
moderation, we maintain our full -year 
growth forecast at 5.3% y/y.

Inflation rates in Indonesia and the 
Philippines are expected to rise, albeit to 
different degrees. Indonesia’s inflation is 
on course to edge up modestly, while in 
the Philippines, stronger pass -through 
from higher oil prices, food and 
transportation costs, as well as imported 
inflation pressures stemming from a 
weaker peso, is likely to push inflation to 
a multi-year high.

Document 398: Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

Threats to European diesel imports: risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased 
risk of price rises

In Europe, the possibility of a US ban on diesel exports has heightened concerns about price trends for this widely used 
fuel1, which has already risen significantly since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran. It has become a major political issue 
on both sides of the Atlantic in the run-up to decisive elections.

A particularly tight diesel market due to a shortage of buffer stocks 
Following the outbreak of the conflict, diesel prices did indeed see some of the sharpest rises on the oil market due to particularly 
tight market conditions. Since the end of February, whilst the price of a barrel of crude has risen by 46%, diesel prices have soared 
by 100% in Europe and by 76% in the United States.

The factors alleviating pressure on the refined products market, and more specifically the diesel market, were more limited than in 
the case of crude oil. Shipments passing through the Strait of Hormuz (around 17% of maritime diesel trade prior to the conf lict) 
have fallen by around 75%. Added to this is the halt in Russian exports due to war damage, which is depriving the diesel market of 
around 12% of its maritime exports. Furthermore, unlike with crude oil, China has not acted as a stabilising  force in the diesel 
market. On the contrary, the energy crisis has led to a halt in Chinese exports of refined products (including diesel) to the  rest of 
Asia from April onwards. The resumption of Chinese exports over the last two months remains fragile, given, in particular, that 
diesel stocks are at their lowest level in 15 months.

The United States has eased these tensions in the diesel market by increasing its exports of refined products (up 25% on average 
in the first half of 2026 compared with the average over the last five years). This rise in exports has required high refinery utilisation 
rate (close to maximum capacity) and, to a lesser extent, a reduction in stocks (-11% compared with the average over the last five 
years as at mid -September for middle distillates 2). All these factors have contributed to tightening the US domestic market and 
pushed retail diesel prices to record levels. Road diesel prices (US average) have risen by 68% since the end of February and by 
74% year-on-year.

A structurally high level of vulnerability in Europe… 
Against this market backdrop, characterised by mounting tensions and very limited room for manoeuvre , Europe would be 
particularly hard hit by a reduction in – or even a complete halt to – US exports. According to Eurostat, imports accounted for 46% 
of European demand for diesel in the first half of 2026, with 40% of these imports coming from the United States. Europe, which

1 Diesel accounts for 17% of the primary energy mix in the EU and 7.3% in the United States. 
2 A category of refined petroleum products comprising mainly diesel and aviation fuel.

### Page 2

has lost a great deal of production capacity in recent years, is operating its refineries at near -maximum capacity. Its room for 
manoeuvre is therefore limited.

… But the risk of a shortage has been ruled out in the short term 
The risk of a shortage, however, appears limited in the short term. The situation regarding European stocks is not comfortable, but 
it is not alarming. Commercial diesel stocks in the ARA region 3 are currently at historically low levels (around 12 million barrels), 
23% below their level at the end of September 2025. As for emergency stocks (EU Emergency Stocks), these are, according to the 
European Commission, at a sufficient level. Eurostat estim ates this figure at 270 million barrels in June 2026 (stable from a year 
earlier), representing approximately 60 days’ worth of EU consumption. Europe imports around 0.9 mb/d of diesel from the United 
States, and emergency stocks would therefore cover around 60 days’ consumption at 4.5 mb/d.

The rise in prices could be significant 
Whatever the outcome of the conflict, diesel prices are likely to continue to rise in the short term. According to our base -case 
scenario, crude oil prices are expected to remain very high in Q4 2026 due to ongoing tensions in the Persian Gulf, whilst a return 
to normal levels of diesel exports from Russia and the Middle East appears unlikely by that time.

Among the factors likely to cause future tensions, the start of the maintenance season in Europe could affect production leve ls, 
whilst demand for diesel in the United States is typically high in the autumn (agricultural harvests and pre -winter storage). I n a 
scenario where tensions remain low, the diesel refining margin (crack spread) could rise by 5% in Q4 compared with its level at the 
end of September 2026, before gradually falling back during 2027. An intermediate scenario (a 10% rise in the crack spread in Q4 
2026) would result in moderate constraints on US diesel exports and a drawdown of European stocks. Finally, an adverse scenario 
(a 35% rise in the crack spread in Q4 2026) would imply severe restrictions on US diesel exports and a delayed European response.

What impact will this have on inflation in the euro area? 
According to our estimates, the rise in diesel prices would exacerbate the expected peak in inflation in the euro area without altering 
its timing. In our baseline scenario for September 2026 (see our recently published EcoPerspective), headline inflation would peak 
at 4.1% y/y in January 2027 before gradually falling back towards 2% by the end of the year. The two least adverse scenarios 
would add only a limited amount to the peak: +0.1 pp in the first scenario (+5% for the crack spread in Q4 compared with the end 
of Q3) and +0.2 pp in the intermediate scenario (+10%), with inflation reaching 4.2% and 4.3% respectively in January 2027. 
However, there is an upside risk in the second scenario linked to the uncertain extent of the spillov er effects. In both cases, the 
main impact would be felt through fuel prices (which quickly reflect rising market prices) and would fade over the course of 2027. 
In the case of the adverse scenario (+35%), the additional inflation would be close to 0.6 pp by early 2027, pushing inflation above 
4.6%. A shock of this magnitude would, in fact, reduce businesses’ ability to absorb rising costs, which would lead to price increases 
across a broader range of goods (food, industrial goods). Subsequently, the effect would only fade gradually.

Pascal Devaux 
Economist, Middle East, Western Balkans – Energy 
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe, Eurozone, International Trade 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

3 Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp, Europe’s main hub for the storage of raw materials.
